class="mb-8 flex flex-col gap-3"><p class="eyebrow text-[10.5px]">Somnia Markets / TypeScript SDK</p><h1 class="display text-[36px] text-fg leading-[1.08]">Perps</h1></header><div class="prose-docs"><p>Perps are live on testnet: <code>BTC/USDSO:USDSO</code> and <code>ETH/USDSO:USDSO</code>, backed by
PerpPool CLOBs riding the same shared OrderBook core as spot and binary.</p>
<h2 id="the-shape">The shape</h2>
<p><code>Market</code> is a three-way discriminated union — <code>SpotMarket | PerpMarket | BinaryMarket</code>, keyed on <code>marketType</code> (guards: <code>isSpotMarket</code> / <code>isPerpMarket</code> /
<code>isBinaryMarket</code>). A <code>PerpMarket</code> is a plain base/quote book plus perp state:
<code>marginBank</code>, <code>initialMarginBps</code>, <code>fundingRate</code>, <code>cumulativeFundingPerUnit</code>,
<code>indexPrice</code>, <code>openInterest</code>, <code>fundingWindowSec</code>, <code>fundingIntervalSec</code>.</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong><code>fundingRate</code> is per CALCULATION WINDOW</strong> (<code>fundingWindowSec</code>, 28800s / 8h on every
live pool) — not per settlement interval and not annualized. Each settlement accrues
<code>rate / n</code> where <code>n = fundingWindowSec / fundingIntervalSec</code>: <strong>8</strong> on every live pool
(3600s settlement). It has been <strong>96</strong> at a 300s cadence, and the same value means a 12x
different per-interval accrual across that boundary — which still reaches anyone reading
indexed history, so read <code>n</code> off each row rather than assuming it. Normalize with the
helpers
(<code>fundingRate8h</code>, <code>fundingRate1h</code>, <code>fundingRatePerInterval</code>, <code>annualizedFundingRate</code>),
never with a hardcoded denominator.</p>
<p><code>openInterest</code> replaced <code>longOpenInterest</code> + <code>shortOpenInterest</code>: the contract keeps ONE
counter because the short side is provably equal in a matched CLOB. The removed pair was
<code>null</code> on every row — the subscription feeding it was dead.</p>
</blockquote>
<ul>
<li><p><strong>Watches and live reads are unchanged.</strong> Perp pools emit the same order
events, so <code>watchMarket(pool)</code>, <code>getLiveSpotOrderBook</code>-style depth,
<code>getLiveFills</code>, <code>getLiveUserOrders</code>, and the React hooks just work. Funding
(<code>FundingUpdated</code>) and open interest (<code>OpenInterestUpdated</code>) stream into the
perp market row live.</p>
</li>
<li><p><strong>Margin, not escrow.</strong> Collateral (USDso) lives cross-margin in the
MarginBank: <code>trader.depositMargin</code> / <code>withdrawMargin</code> move it;
<code>trader.placePerpOrder</code> (or plain <code>createOrder</code> on a perp symbol) locks
margin from that balance — no per-order token approval.</p>
</li>
<li><p><strong>Re-quoting a ladder? Amend it in one transaction.</strong> <code>trader.amendOrders</code> re-prices
N orders in one transaction (see SPOT.md for how each pool version applies it and
what a <code>0n</code> id means). On a perp pool it needs no token fields at all — margin comes from the
MarginBank, so there is no escrow to approve.</p>
</li>
<li><p><strong>Builder attribution.</strong> <code>placePerpOrder</code> takes optional <code>builder</code> /
<code>builderFeeBpsTimes1k</code> (alongside its existing <code>expireTimestampNs</code>), both
defaulting to no attribution. A non-zero fee needs a prior
<code>trader.approveBuilder({ pool, builder, maxFeeBpsTimes1k })</code> and must stay
within <code>trader.getMaxBuilderFeeBpsTimes1k(pool)</code> — read it rather than assuming it,
since it is owner-updatable on a PerpPool and the rail is off while it is 0.
Perp pools implement the same builder calls as binary ones, but <strong>the approval
is per pool</strong>: approving a builder on one pool grants nothing on another, and
an unapproved placement reverts <code>BuilderFeeExceedsApproval</code> (a PerpPool has no
separate &quot;not approved&quot; check — an absent approval is simply a zero cap).</p>
</li>
<li><p><strong>Positions are on-chain reads</strong>, not indexed rows:
<code>client.getPerpPosition({ marginBank, account, pool })</code> and
<code>client.getMarginAccount(marginBank, account)</code> — or the unified
<code>exchange.fetchPositions()</code>. Live pricing/funding comes from
<code>client.getPerpState(pool)</code> / <code>exchange.fetchFundingRate(symbol)</code>.</p>
</li>
<li><p><strong>MONITORING a mark feed is a different read.</strong> <code>client.getPerpFeedStatus(pool)</code>
answers whether the mark is live and how much open interest rides on it.
<code>getPerpState</code> batches with <code>allowFailure: false</code> and one of its legs is a bare
<code>IOracle.getPrice()</code>, so a dead or rotated oracle rejects the whole read — losing
the verdict in the one case it matters most. <code>getPerpFeedStatus</code> keeps the mark
verdict and open interest as a pair no oracle can revert, and degrades the index
timestamp to <code>undefined</code> instead. Use <code>getPerpState</code> when you want the full
pricing and funding picture and a dead oracle should fail the call.</p>
</li>
<li><p><strong>…except when you want them all at once.</strong> <code>client.listPerpPositions(account)</code>
returns every pool&#39;s position in ONE indexer round-trip instead of a chain read
per market — the right read for a positions <em>table</em>. It is a snapshot as of each
row&#39;s <code>updatedAtBlock</code> and is <strong>not marked to market</strong>: unrealized PnL,
liquidation price and margin health still need the chain reads above. Two things
the shape will not let you get wrong, both documented on the type:
<code>size</code> is <strong>signed</strong> (the entity stores magnitude and direction separately; they
are folded back together here so a short can&#39;t read as a long), and the entry
funding index is <strong>absent by design</strong> — it is not on the schema production
serves, so it waits on the next reindex — and anything funding-sensitive belongs
on <code>getPerpPosition</code>. Fully-closed positions are
excluded unless you pass <code>includeFlat</code> — upserted rows are never deleted, so they
linger at size 0 forever.</p>
</li>
<li><p><strong>Every account at once.</strong> <code>client.getPerpPositionsPage({ pool, minSize, includeFlat, cursor })</code>
reads all accounts&#39; positions with <code>entryFundingIndex</code>. Each page is one request
of up to 1000 rows in <code>id</code> order. Pass <code>nextCursor</code> back as <code>cursor</code> until it is <code>null</code>.</p>
</li>
<li><p><strong>Margin health is a chain read too.</strong> <code>getMarginAccount</code> now also returns the
requirements + status (<code>imReq</code> / <code>mmReq</code> / <code>cmReq</code> / <code>marginStatus</code>, from
<code>MarginBank.getAccountHealth</code> + <code>getMarginStatus</code>);
<code>client.getAccountHealth(marginBank, account)</code> is the lighter standalone read
when only health matters, and <code>client.getLiquidationPrice({ marginBank, pool, account })</code> estimates the mark at which this pool&#39;s move alone would trip
maintenance (null when flat). <code>MarginStatus</code> / <code>MARGIN_STATUS</code> /
<code>AccountHealth</code> are exported; <code>exchange.fetchPositions()</code> now fills
<code>UnifiedPosition.liquidationPrice</code>.</p>
</li>
<li><p><strong>Account equity for scoring is one read.</strong>
<code>client.getAccountEquity(account, { scope: &quot;perps&quot;, marginBank })</code> returns <code>equity</code>,
<code>lockedCollateral</code>, their sum <code>totalEquity</code> and the block they were read at.
<code>equity</code> counts free collateral only, and a resting order moves collateral to
<code>lockedCollateral</code>, so score on <code>totalEquity</code>. The read has no history: store a
snapshot at the start and at the end of the period. A market with no fresh mark
price makes the read throw. Pass <code>blockNumber</code> to read at a fixed block instead of
the head. <code>client.listAccountEquities(accounts, params)</code> reads a cohort at one block
in batched requests; an account that cannot be priced comes back as
<code>{ priceable: false }</code> and does not fail the rest. The <code>&quot;spot&quot;</code> and <code>&quot;overall&quot;</code> scopes take no
<code>marginBank</code> and throw <code>NotSupportedError</code>.</p>
<p>The change between two snapshots includes money that moved in or out of the
account. Remove those flows before you call the change a trading gain. Each snapshot
carries <code>asOfBlock</code>, and the read at block <code>a</code> already includes every flow in block
<code>a</code>, so the flows to remove are those in blocks <code>a + 1</code> to <code>b</code>, both included. When
both snapshots were read at the same block, there are no flows between them: skip the
reads, because <code>a + 1</code> to <code>a</code> is an inverted range and is rejected. Three indexed reads
list the flows, and each takes the same inclusive <code>sinceBlock</code> / <code>toBlock</code>:</p>
<table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Read</th>
<th>Rows to count</th>
<th>Sign</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody><tr>
<td><code>getMarginEvents</code></td>
<td><code>Deposit</code>, <code>Credited</code> (voucher grant)</td>
<td>in</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><code>getMarginEvents</code></td>
<td><code>Withdraw</code>, <code>CreditReclaimed</code> (voucher sweep)</td>
<td>out</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><code>listPerpMarginPulls</code></td>
<td>every row, <code>OwnWallet</code> and <code>Main</code></td>
<td>in</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><code>listPerpMainFundingEvents</code></td>
<td><code>kind: &quot;Returned&quot;</code>: principal sent to a main</td>
<td>out</td>
</tr>
</tbody></table>
<p>Leave out <code>Locked</code> / <code>Unlocked</code> (collateral moving between the account&#39;s free and
order-locked balance) and the main-funding <code>Funded</code> and <code>Settled</code> rows: a <code>Funded</code> row
is the same money as a <code>Main</code> pull, and a <code>Settled</code> row moved none.</p>
<p>The procedure removes the money the account&#39;s owner moved in or out. Two other credits
reach the balance without a trade by the account, and the procedure counts them as
trading gain: builder fees that other traders pay to the account as a builder
(<code>listPerpFees({ builder: account, kind: &quot;BuilderFee&quot; })</code>), and the insurance fund
covering a liquidated account&#39;s negative balance (<code>listLiquidations</code> kind
<code>BadDebtAbsorbed</code>, field <code>insuranceCovered</code>). A competition that must remove them reads
those lists too. Neither takes a block range. A liquidation row carries its
<code>blockNumber</code>, so keep the rows in <code>a + 1</code> to <code>b</code>. A fee row carries only a
<code>timestamp</code>, so a builder fee close to either snapshot cannot be placed exactly.
Everything else that changes equity (fills, fees, funding, liquidations) is trading.</p>
<p>Page each read until a short page. The margin events come back in block, then log
index order, and the two pull ledgers in block, then id order, so the reads are
stable: run the procedure twice and it returns the same rows and the same net. Wait
until the indexer has processed block <code>b</code> before you net, or the newest flows are
missing: <code>client.waitForIndexedBlock(chainId, end.asOfBlock)</code> does that.</p>
<div class="code-copy"><pre><code class="language-ts">const start = await client.getAccountEquity(account, { scope: &quot;perps&quot;, marginBank });
// ... one week later
const end = await client.getAccountEquity(account, { scope: &quot;perps&quot;, marginBank });
// Flows in the end block are already in the end equity: read none until they are indexed.
await client.waitForIndexedBlock(client.config.chain.id, end.asOfBlock);

async function all&lt;T&gt;(read: (page: { limit: number; offset: number }) =&gt; Promise&lt;T[]&gt;): Promise&lt;T[]&gt; {
  const rows: T[] = [];
  for (let offset = 0; ; offset += 200) {
    const page = await read({ limit: 200, offset });
    rows.push(...page);
    if (page.length &lt; 200) return rows;
  }
}

let flows = 0n;
// Two reads at one block have no flows between them.
if (end.asOfBlock &gt; start.asOfBlock) {
  const range = { sinceBlock: start.asOfBlock + 1n, toBlock: end.asOfBlock };
  const events = await all((page) =&gt;
    client.getMarginEvents(account, {
      kinds: [&quot;Deposit&quot;, &quot;Credited&quot;, &quot;Withdraw&quot;, &quot;CreditReclaimed&quot;],
      ...range,
      ...page,
    }),
  );
  const pulls = await all((page) =&gt; client.listPerpMarginPulls({ account, ...range, ...page }));
  const returned = await all((page) =&gt;
    client.listPerpMainFundingEvents({ account, kind: &quot;Returned&quot;, ...range, ...page }),
  );
  for (const e of events)
    flows += e.kind === &quot;Deposit&quot; || e.kind === &quot;Credited&quot; ? BigInt(e.amount) : -BigInt(e.amount);
  for (const p of pulls) flows += BigInt(p.amount);
  for (const r of returned) flows -= BigInt(r.amount ?? &quot;0&quot;);
}

const tradingGain = end.totalEquity - start.totalEquity - flows;
</code></pre><button type="button" class="code-copy-btn" data-copy-code aria-live="polite">Copy</button></div>
<p>A competition decides for itself which of these flows disqualify an entrant. The
voucher campaign below explains when an account can receive outside money.</p>
</li>
<li><p><strong>Liquidation price: both sides of the inequality move with the mark.</strong> Liquidation
begins where <code>equity == mmReq</code>, and <code>mmReq</code> is recomputed on the <em>current</em> mark
(<code>ceil(|size| × mark × mmBps / (oneBase × 10000))</code>), so it shrinks under a falling
long and grows under a rising short. Solving <code>equity(p) == mmReq(p)</code> therefore carries
a <code>10000 ∓ mmBps</code> factor:</p>
<div class="code-copy"><pre><code class="language-text">long    p = mark − (equity − mmReq) × oneBase × 10000 / (|size| × (10000 − mmBps))
short   p = mark + (equity − mmReq) × oneBase × 10000 / (|size| × (10000 + mmBps))
</code></pre><button type="button" class="code-copy-btn" data-copy-code aria-live="polite">Copy</button></div>
<p><code>perpLiquidationPrice(...)</code> is that solve, exported as a pure function — no client, no
block — so you can re-run it against a live store or a hypothetical. Both
<code>getLiquidationPrice</code> and <code>previewPerpLiquidationPrice</code> go through it, which is why
they cannot disagree on identical inputs. It is a <strong>single-market</strong> solve: other
markets&#39; contributions are held at the value baked into <code>equity</code>/<code>mmReq</code>, so a
correlated move across several markets liquidates sooner.</p>
</li>
<li><p><strong>Where would an order put my liquidation price?</strong>
<code>client.previewPerpLiquidationPrice({ pool, marginBank, account, isBid, quantity, price, asMaker? })</code> answers that for an order not yet placed, returning
<code>currentLiquidationPrice</code> beside <code>projectedLiquidationPrice</code> so a form can show the
move. It ports all four of <code>MarginBank.settleTrade</code>&#39;s cases — open, increase (floored
VWAP entry), reduce/close (realized PnL at the fill price, entry untouched), and flip
(old side closed, remainder re-opened) — and charges the fill&#39;s fee, defaulting to the
taker rate. A reduce moves the price <em>away</em> from the mark and an add moves it <em>closer</em>,
so the four cases are not interchangeable.</p>
<p>Note what it deliberately does <strong>not</strong> do: it applies the whole quantity rather than
splitting it against <code>getReducingCapacity</code> (that split governs the collateral <em>lock</em>,
not the position), and it does not judge whether the order would be accepted — that is
<code>previewPerpOrderMargin</code>&#39;s job. An unpriceable market returns <code>{ priceable: false }</code>
rather than reverting, so an order form can still render.</p>
</li>
<li><p><strong>How much can I actually place?</strong> <code>client.getMaxPerpOrderSize({ pool, marginBank, account, isBid, price })</code> — what a <strong>Max</strong> button should call. The inverse of
<code>previewPerpOrderMargin</code>, which the protocol does not offer.</p>
<p>It does not re-derive the sizing rule; it <strong>binary-searches the forward one</strong>, so the
two cannot disagree. That matters because a max computed by a second, subtly different
rule reverts on placement — and the term such a rule most often drops is the adverse
mark-to-entry reserve, which on a 10%-above-mark bid cuts the affordable size by
roughly two thirds. <code>maxQuantity</code> is aligned <strong>down</strong> to the pool&#39;s lot grid.</p>
<p><strong>Check <code>placeable</code>.</strong> A size below the pool&#39;s <code>minQuantity</code> is a revert, not a small
order. <code>limitedBy</code> names the binding gate — <code>&quot;collateral&quot;</code>, <code>&quot;initialMargin&quot;</code>,
<code>&quot;maxPositionSize&quot;</code> or <code>&quot;voucherBlocked&quot;</code>. Market-wide <code>maxOpenInterest</code> is deliberately
not modelled: it is enforced at fill against a total every other trader moves, so no
client-side number can be right about it for long. Nor is book depth — this is a
placement limit, not a liquidity one. Nor are the two non-margin gates that reject the
whole order rather than shrinking it: a close-only market (<code>PerpPool.isRestricted()</code>)
and isolated-margin confinement.</p>
<p><strong>Pass <code>autoPull: true</code> when the transaction sender will be the order owner.</strong> The pool
tops the in-bank balance up from the owner&#39;s wallet before it locks (<code>_onOrderPlaced</code> →
<code>MarginBank.quoteOrderTopUp</code> → <code>depositFor</code>), so an account with an empty bank and a
funded, approved wallet goes from a max of <code>0n</code> to whatever the wallet funds. `msg.sender</p>
</li>
</ul>
<p>== order.owner<code>is the pool&#39;s entire gate, and only the caller knows who will send —   hence opt-in. Leave it off for</code>placeOrderFor`, an operator grant or the stop registry,
  where no pull happens.</p>
<p>  With it on, <code>topUpRequired</code> is the wallet spend to show beside the size, and <code>limitedBy</code>
  gains <code>&quot;walletBalance&quot;</code> / <code>&quot;walletAllowance&quot;</code> — different shortfalls with different
  fixes, so don&#39;t collapse them. <code>&quot;restricted&quot;</code> and <code>&quot;isolated&quot;</code> name the two gates that
  reject the whole order rather than resizing it.</p>
<p>  <strong>A linked child is funded by two wallets, and both count.</strong> The pool spends the
  owner&#39;s own wallet first and takes the residual from the main it is linked to, so
  <code>autoPull</code> reads <code>MarginBank.quoteFundingPayer</code> and adds the main&#39;s
  <code>quoteWalletCapacity</code> as a second leg. That matters most for the account the rail
  exists for: an isolated sub-account is meant to hold nothing, and sized off its own
  wallet alone it reads as unable to place anything at all.</p>
<p>  <code>fundingPayer</code> names the main <strong>eligible</strong> to fund a shortfall, or is <code>null</code> when the
  account funds itself — unlinked, a main itself, or the rail dormant on this deployment.
  Eligibility is not a debit: it resolves for every linked account, including an order
  needing no top-up and one whose own wallet covers the whole pull. <code>ownWalletPull</code> and
  <code>mainWalletPull</code> split <code>topUpRequired</code> between the two wallets in the order the pool
  spends them, and <strong>only <code>mainWalletPull &gt; 0n</code> proves a second wallet actually moves</strong> —
  that is the figure to show before the trader signs.</p>
<p>  Read <code>topUpRequired</code> as the total REQUESTED, not one wallet&#39;s spend. Showing it as the
  child&#39;s debit over-states it by exactly the main&#39;s leg.</p>
<p>  <code>limitedBy</code> gains <code>&quot;mainWallet&quot;</code>, reported ahead of the two <code>wallet*</code> values whenever a
  main is in play: at a main-funded ceiling the child&#39;s own capacity is fully consumed,
  so <code>&quot;walletBalance&quot;</code> would also be literally true and would send a trader to fund a
  sub-account that is empty by design. It names the <strong>preferred</strong> remedy, not the only
  one — the two capacities add, so raising the child&#39;s balance or allowance lifts the
  ceiling just as well.</p>
<p>  <strong>A partial pull is not a rejection on this path.</strong> The unlinked pool asks for a fixed
  <code>topUpRequired</code> and the token reverts if the wallet is short. Both linked legs are
  <code>min(...)</code>-sized and neither can revert, so the pool pulls what it can and the margin
  gates judge the result — and <code>topUpRequired</code> includes the fee headroom, which is a
  reserve rather than part of the lock. So capacities that cover the lock but not the
  headroom fund an order the chain accepts, and <code>walletCoversTopUp</code> is informational
  there rather than a gate: read a <code>false</code> as &quot;the position may be born close to its own
  initial margin&quot;, not as &quot;this will be rejected&quot;.</p>
<p>  One case withholds the main&#39;s leg entirely. <code>MarginBank.depositForFromMain</code> allows one
  payer at a time, so while the child still owes a <strong>prior</strong> payer the pull reverts
  <code>PriorFundingPayerOutstanding</code> — reachable in one ordinary sequence: funded by main A,
  unlink, re-link to main B. <code>mainFundingBlocked</code> reports it and <code>limitedBy</code> says
  <code>&quot;mainFundingBlocked&quot;</code>; the fix is neither wallet but
  <code>trader.repayPerpMainFunding</code> against the old claim.</p>
<p>  It costs one extra read for an unlinked account and four for a linked one. The main&#39;s
  capacity arrives as a single <code>min(balance, allowance)</code>, which is all the bank exposes
  for another wallet, so a main-side shortfall does not say which of the two bound; read
  the main&#39;s token balance and allowance directly if you need that.</p>
<p>  Note the max is the top of the <strong>contiguous</strong> placeable region. Auto-pull makes the
  initial-margin gate non-monotone in quantity — it reduces to <code>(equity − unlocked) + feeHeadroom ≥ imRequirement</code>, whose only size-dependent term grows — so an account whose
  existing positions sit below their own initial margin can be rejected at a middling size
  and accepted at a much larger one. <code>previewPerpOrderMargin</code> reports that faithfully; the
  max deliberately does not offer sizes out of the disconnected region, because a slider
  has to be placeable at every value below its maximum.</p>
<ul>
<li><p><strong>What do I get if I close?</strong> <code>client.previewPerpClosePnl({ pool, marginBank, account, quantity?, price?, asMaker? })</code> — backs a close modal. Omit <code>quantity</code> for the whole
position; <code>price</code> defaults to the mark, which is the right estimate for a market close.</p>
<p>Two things it gets right that a hand-derived figure usually does not, and <strong>both are
silent</strong> — the close succeeds, the number shown was just wrong:</p>
<ol>
<li><strong>The size is aligned down to the lot grid first.</strong> &quot;Close all&quot; on a position that
is not a lot multiple leaves a remainder open. A modal reporting the position as
flat is wrong, and it reads as a bug in the close button.</li>
<li><strong>Funding settles on the whole position, not the closed share.</strong> <code>settleTrade</code> calls
<code>_settleFundingWithValues</code> <em>before</em> it touches the position, and that uses the full
<code>pos.size</code>. So a 10% close settles 100% of the accrued funding; pro-rating it — the
intuitive move — under-states the cash impact by the other 90%.</li>
</ol>
<p><code>netProceeds</code> is the number to show: <code>realizedPnl − fundingSettled − fee</code>, with
<code>fundingSettled</code> positive when the account pays. <code>realizedPnl</code> <strong>floors</strong> toward −∞
(<code>_realizedPnlForClose</code>), which is the opposite of the truncation unrealized PnL uses —
the same inputs give <code>-1</code> here and <code>0</code> from <code>getPerpPositionAnalytics</code>, and both are
correct.</p>
<p><code>placeable</code> is the pool minimum and nothing else. A close is only purely reducing up to
<code>_reducingCapacity</code> — <code>|size|</code> minus what is already resting on the reducing side — so
closing out while a reduce order is down leaves an increasing remainder that locks
collateral and must clear <code>meetsIMForOrder</code>. Read <code>previewPerpOrderMargin</code> beside this
one on an account with resting orders. <code>fee</code> is the pool&#39;s own maker/taker rate; a
builder fee attached at placement is charged on the same notional and lands on top.</p>
</li>
<li><p><strong>Closing when the book is thin.</strong> A close sent with a fixed price band fills only what
rests inside that band. <code>client.quotePerpClose</code> tells you how much that is before you send.
It walks the opposite side of the live book in raw units:</p>
<div class="code-copy"><pre><code class="language-ts">const q = await client.quotePerpClose({ pool, marginBank, account });
if (q.priceable &amp;&amp; q.fillableSize &lt; q.quantity) {
  // Only q.fillableSize fills within the pool&#39;s band.
  // q.bandToFillAll is the band that fills it all, or null if the book is too thin.
  // q.oppositeSideEmpty is true when nothing rests on the other side.
}
</code></pre><button type="button" class="code-copy-btn" data-copy-code aria-live="polite">Copy</button></div>
<p>Pass <code>bandBps</code> to quote the band you will send, for example <code>50n</code> for 0.5%. The default
and the upper limit is the pool&#39;s own band (<code>getCircuitBreakerParameters().maxPriceMovementBps</code>),
measured from the mark, or from the last good mark while the feed is stale. A limit close
at <code>limitPrice</code> can rest its unfilled part without reverting <code>OrderPriceOutsideBand</code>. Pass
<code>isBid</code> and <code>quantity</code> instead of <code>account</code> to quote a bare size. On the exchange surface,
<code>exchange.quotePerpClose(symbol)</code> quotes the connected wallet&#39;s position.</p>
</li>
<li><p><strong>What is this position actually doing?</strong> <code>client.getPerpPositionAnalytics({ marginBank, pool, account })</code> for one, <code>client.listPerpPositionAnalytics({ marginBank, account })</code>
for a positions table. Two reads for one position, <code>1 + 2n</code> for the table, all pinned
to one block.</p>
<p>This is the split <code>getAccountHealth</code> cannot give you: it returns <strong>one</strong> equity figure
for the whole account, with every market&#39;s PnL and funding already summed and netted
together, so a two-position trader cannot see which position carries the loss and
cannot see funding at all. Back apart:</p>
<table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Field</th>
<th>Means</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody><tr>
<td><code>unrealizedPnl</code></td>
<td><code>(mark − entry) × size / oneBase</code> — price only, <strong>excludes</strong> funding</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><code>accruedFunding</code></td>
<td>funding <strong>owed</strong> since entry; <strong>positive means you pay</strong></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><code>equityContribution</code></td>
<td><code>unrealizedPnl − accruedFunding</code> — what this position adds to equity</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><code>notional</code></td>
<td><code>|size| × mark / oneBase</code></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><code>initialMarginRequirement</code></td>
<td>&quot;position margin&quot; — the only per-position margin the protocol defines</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><code>maintenanceMarginRequirement</code> / <code>closeOutMarginRequirement</code></td>
<td>the liquidation and takeover thresholds&#39; shares</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><code>returnOnMarginBps</code></td>
<td><code>equityContribution</code> over the initial requirement, <strong>net of funding</strong>; <code>null</code> when flat</td>
</tr>
</tbody></table>
<p>A direct port of <code>MarginBank._computePositionMetrics</code> and <code>_marketHealthFromSnapshot</code>,
which is what lets the rows re-sum to the bank&#39;s own equity to the wei — a test pins
that. <strong>The two roundings differ and are not interchangeable</strong>: unrealized PnL
truncates toward zero, while funding ceils toward +∞ so a payer never underpays. For a
negative quotient that is the opposite of rounding the magnitude up.</p>
<p><code>accruedFunding</code> uses the pool&#39;s <em>projected</em> cumulative index, so it includes intervals
no one has settled yet — settlement is permissionless and lazy, and measuring against
the settled index under-reports what the account already owes. An unpriceable market
comes back as <code>{ priceable: false }</code> rather than throwing, so one dead feed costs one
row rather than the page. <code>initialMarginRequirement</code> uses the market&#39;s IMF only: an
account leverage setting raises the bar for a <em>new</em> order but never appears in health,
so use <code>previewPerpOrderMargin</code> for the order-gating figure. The pure core is exported
as <code>perpPositionAnalytics(...)</code> — no client, no block.</p>
</li>
<li><p><strong>How levered am I?</strong> <code>client.getPerpLeverage({ marginBank, pool, account })</code>.
<strong>The protocol has no leverage view to call</strong> — <code>getMaxLeverage</code> /
<code>getMaxLeverageLimit</code> / <code>getVoucherLeverageCap</code> are all <em>cap configuration</em> and none of
them measures a position — so this derives it from mark notional over equity, and
returns every denominator rather than picking one:</p>
<table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Field</th>
<th>Means</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody><tr>
<td><code>positionLeverageBps</code></td>
<td>this position&#39;s notional over account equity</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><code>accountLeverageBps</code></td>
<td><strong>Σ</strong> notional over account equity — the figure that governs risk under cross margin</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><code>marketMaxLeverageBps</code></td>
<td><code>10000² / effectiveImfBps</code> — the most this market will open at, at <em>live</em> OI-scaled IMF</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><code>accountMaxLeverageX</code></td>
<td>the account&#39;s own per-market cap, <code>0</code> when unset (what <code>setPerpLeverage</code> writes, finally readable)</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><code>protocolMaxLeverageX</code></td>
<td>the ceiling that clamps it</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><code>creditFloor</code></td>
<td>the account&#39;s credit-voucher floor; <code>0n</code> on an ordinary account, and the switch that arms the two below</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><code>voucherLeverageCapX</code></td>
<td>what a voucher account is confined to when it <strong>increases</strong>; <code>0</code> is a block, not &quot;uncapped&quot;</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><code>voucherMarketAllowed</code></td>
<td>whether this market is on the voucher allowlist</td>
</tr>
</tbody></table>
<p>The ceilings are <strong>not</strong> collapsed into one number, and they do not compose by taking a
minimum. A voucher cap <em>replaces</em> an unset or looser <code>accountMaxLeverageX</code> before
<code>protocolMaxLeverageX</code> clamps the result, and it applies only to a position increase —
<code>MarginBank._meetsIM</code> gates the whole voucher branch on <code>additionalSize &gt; 0</code>, so a
voucher holder can always close out or place a stop, even on a market since removed from
the allowlist. The load-bearing half is that a voucher turns an <em>unset</em> account cap into
an enforced one: <code>accountMaxLeverageX === 0</code> means &quot;no cap&quot; on an ordinary account and
&quot;confined to <code>voucherLeverageCapX</code>&quot; on a voucher one. For whether a <em>specific</em> order
passes, use <code>previewPerpOrderMargin</code>, which applies all of it and reports
<code>voucherBlocked</code>.</p>
<p>Every ratio is <strong>bps of 1x</strong> (<code>10_000</code> = 1.00x, <code>200_000</code> = 20x), matching the
protocol&#39;s own unit for every margin figure. Position and account leverage are <code>null</code>
on non-positive equity — an account with no equity left is insolvent, not infinitely
levered; read <code>marginStatus</code> for that. <code>accountNotional</code> costs two extra reads per
<em>other</em> active market (the MarginBank exposes no aggregate, and <code>imReq</code> can&#39;t be
inverted because each market applies its own IMF), so a single-market account pays
nothing extra.</p>
</li>
<li><p><strong>Liquidation-keeper reads — who holds, and what a bankrupt position is worth.</strong>
The MarginBank keeps a per-(pool, side) holder array, so a keeper can find every
open position from head state alone — no off-chain indexer:</p>
<div class="code-copy"><pre><code class="language-ts">const { holders, asOfBlock } = await client.getPerpSideHolders({ marginBank, pool, isLong: true });
const prices = await Promise.all(
  // Priced at the SNAPSHOT&#39;s block — at head, a holder that closed since
  // the enumeration would revert NoOpenPosition and reject the sweep.
  holders.map((h) =&gt; client.getBankruptcyPrice({ marginBank, account: h, pool }, { blockNumber: asOfBlock })),
);
</code></pre><button type="button" class="code-copy-btn" data-copy-code aria-live="polite">Copy</button></div>
<p><code>getPerpSideHolders</code> pages through the bank&#39;s bounded slice view (many holders per
round-trip) with every page pinned to ONE block; feed <code>asOfBlock</code> into
<code>getBankruptcyPrice</code>&#39;s <code>blockNumber</code> option (and into the other side&#39;s call) to
keep a sweep on one consistent snapshot. The other position/health reads answer
at head only.</p>
<p><code>getBankruptcyPrice</code> is the <strong>contract&#39;s own figure</strong> — the price at which the
position&#39;s allocated share of the account&#39;s equity is exhausted — and it is a
different quantity from <code>getLiquidationPrice</code>, not a better version of it:
<code>getLiquidationPrice</code> is the SDK&#39;s client-side estimate of where liquidation
<em>triggers</em> (use it for UI and monitoring), while <code>getBankruptcyPrice</code> is what the
contract computes a bankrupt position to be worth (use it for anything that
settles or bids — a keeper pays against this number, and a client-side estimate
can drift from contract rounding). It reverts rather than returning a sentinel:
<code>ContractRevertError</code> with <code>errorName: &quot;NoOpenPosition&quot;</code> when the account is flat
in that pool (branch on <code>errorName</code>, never message text).</p>
</li>
<li><p><strong>The ADL queue — where a position stands if a liquidation reaches ADL.</strong>
Auto-deleveraging (ADL) is the last stage of a liquidation that the bidders and the
insurance fund cannot cover. The engine closes profitable positions on the other
side of the bankrupt&#39;s market, in the order that its ranker contract decides.
<code>client.perpAdlQueue</code> reads that order for one side of one market at one block, and
follows it as it changes:</p>
<div class="code-copy"><pre><code class="language-ts">const stop = client.perpAdlQueue.subscribe({ marginBank, pool, isLong: true }, (view) =&gt; {
  if (view.status !== &quot;ready&quot; &amp;&amp; view.status !== &quot;stale&quot;) return;
  const { queue } = view.data;
  if (queue.regime !== &quot;ranked&quot;) return;
  const candidates = queue.holders.filter((h) =&gt; h.eligibility === &quot;candidate&quot;);
  for (const h of candidates) console.log(h.account, `${h.adlRank}/${candidates.length}`, h.positionLeverageBps);
});
</code></pre><button type="button" class="code-copy-btn" data-copy-code aria-live="polite">Copy</button></div>
<p>The read follows the engine. It reads the ranker and the scan cap from the engine
at the pinned block. A side with more holders than the scan cap is walked in storage
order, so the read returns <code>regime: &quot;storageOrder&quot;</code> and no order. A holder is a
candidate when its position is in profit at the mark, its account is priceable, and
its equity is positive. These are the rules of the engine&#39;s claw-back arm, which it
takes when the bankrupt account&#39;s realized balance is negative. When the ranker
reverts or returns a bad order, the engine uses holder-list order. The read gives
that order and sets <code>orderSource</code> to <code>&quot;rankerReverted&quot;</code> or <code>&quot;rankerInvalid&quot;</code>.</p>
<p>The live view reads the side again when the MarginBank reports a change that can
move the queue: any trader&#39;s fill, close, liquidation, takeover, forced settlement,
ADL or funding settlement on this market, and any account&#39;s deposit, withdrawal,
credit, linked funding or close-out payment, your own included. It also reads every 30 s, which picks
up what moves the order with no followed event: a price move, a holder&#39;s equity
changing on another market, and order placement and cancellation. Each order locks
or releases its margin, which moves the trader&#39;s equity and so its place, but the
bank emits them several times a second, so they are not followed. <code>getSnapshot</code>
reads once, unless a live view of the side is ready: then it returns that view.</p>
<p>The queue is an estimate for an ADL on this market alone. A running ADL session
continues from its own cursor and prices. One liquidation call shares its scan
budget across the bankrupt account&#39;s markets. On the burn arm, when the bankrupt
account&#39;s realized balance is not negative, the engine admits any position that is
not at a loss at the bankruptcy price and does not check equity, so a holder with no
equity that is not at a loss there is closed first. Do not tell a trader that a position cannot be
auto-deleveraged.</p>
</li>
<li><p><strong>Protocol state — is the stack wired and solvent.</strong> The plane below any one account
or market, mirroring what the protocol repo&#39;s <code>perps:state</code> / <code>perps:health</code> ops
tasks read:</p>
<div class="code-copy"><pre><code class="language-ts">const cfg = await client.getPerpSystemConfig(marginBank); // the address book
const fund = await client.getInsuranceFundState(cfg.insuranceFund);
const eng = await client.getLiquidationEngineConfig(cfg.liquidationEngine);
</code></pre><button type="button" class="code-copy-btn" data-copy-code aria-live="polite">Copy</button></div>
<p><code>getPerpSystemConfig</code> is the entry point: every other contract in the plane is
reachable from it, so nothing is hardcoded per chain, and it carries <code>fullyWired</code> —
a single flag for &quot;some part of this stack is half-configured&quot;, which is the state
where liquidation and settlement degrade silently rather than reverting.</p>
<p>Point the other two at the addresses it returns. <code>liquidationEngine</code> is the <strong>proxy</strong>:
an implementation address answers with unset defaults (zero bidders, zero penalty),
which reads as a configured-but-idle engine rather than the wrong address.
<code>bidderCount === 0n</code> is itself operational — with no registered backstop bidders the
takeover stage has nobody to take a position over, so the waterfall reaches ADL
sooner than the configuration implies.</p>
<p>For account-level health when a feed may be down: <code>tryGetPerpAccountEquity</code> returns
<code>null</code> rather than reverting (null is &quot;not computable&quot;, never &quot;zero&quot;), and
<code>getPerpCollateralBasis</code> is a solvency floor that reads one storage pair and cannot
revert at all.</p>
</li>
<li><p><strong>Take-profit / stop-loss listing.</strong> <code>client.listPerpStopOrders({ account?, pool?, status? })</code>.
The PerpStopOrderRegistry keeps pending orders in private storage behind no
enumeration getter, so there is no chain read that answers &quot;what stops do I have&quot; —
creation and triggering both work, but without this a trader cannot see, price or
cancel what they created. It is indexed, and there is no chain fallback.</p>
<p>One call covers every scope: <code>{ account }</code> for a trader&#39;s working stops (default
status <code>PENDING</code>), <code>{ pool }</code> with no account for a market&#39;s whole pending book, and
<code>status</code> for history. <code>account</code> is optional deliberately — a market-wide view of
what will fire is a legitimate monitoring read.</p>
<p>Read <code>dropReason</code> before calling a <code>TRIGGER_FAILED</code> order a failure: <code>ReduceOnly*</code>
means the stop was overtaken by events (position already closed, flipped, or below
the minimum), which is ordinary; only <code>PlacementFailed</code> is a rejection. SOMI is
consumed on every fire either way.</p>
<p>A listed row has no <code>limitPrice</code>. Fetch it for live stops with
<code>client.getPerpStopOrders(refs)</code>.</p>
</li>
<li><p><strong>The registry may be holding SOMI for you.</strong> Placing a perp stop pre-pays the
trigger gas in SOMI. Cancelling refunds it by direct transfer — but when that
transfer <em>fails</em>, the registry credits an unclaimed balance instead and emits
<code>SomiRefundFailed</code>. That is the ordinary outcome for a contract owner with no payable
receiver (most multisigs and smart accounts). A registry wind-down credits the same
balance to <strong>every</strong> owner, EOAs included.</p>
<div class="code-copy"><pre><code class="language-ts">const owed = await client.getUnclaimedPerpStopSomi({ registry: perp.stopRegistry, account });
if (owed &gt; 0n) await trader.claimPerpStopSomi({ registry: perp.stopRegistry });
</code></pre><button type="button" class="code-copy-btn" data-copy-code aria-live="polite">Copy</button></div>
<p>Read before claiming — <code>claimSomi</code> reverts <code>NothingToClaim</code> on a zero balance, and
the payout is a plain native transfer to the caller, so an owner that still cannot
receive native reverts <code>WithdrawalFailed</code> and the balance stays put. Do not assume an
EOA is owed nothing: the wind-down path reaches them too. Note the trigger path is the
opposite of a refund — SOMI is consumed on every fire and never returned.</p>
</li>
<li><p><strong>Batching writes that have to be atomic.</strong> One SDK write is one transaction, which
is wrong for a flow that is only safe as one — an order with TP/SL attached (sent
separately, the order can fill and sit with no stop on it), approve + deposit,
withdraw + forward to the wallet. <code>trader.buildPlacePerpStopOrder</code>,
<code>buildCancelPerpStopOrder(s)</code>, <code>buildDepositMargin</code> and <code>buildWithdrawMargin</code> take the
same parameters as their sending twins and return the unsigned call
(<code>{ to, data, value, description }</code>) for you to pack into one UserOp / Safe batch /
multicall.</p>
<p>Two gotchas. Approvals come back rather than going out — <code>operatorApproval</code> on a stop,
<code>approval</code> on a deposit — and must execute <strong>before</strong> the call they enable; the perp
stop&#39;s grant especially, because without it the trigger reverts and the prepaid SOMI is
spent having placed nothing. And no ids come back, because you hold the receipt: read
them with <code>decodePerpStopOrderIds(receipt.logs, registry)</code>.</p>
</li>
<li><p><strong>Where the registry address comes from.</strong> Every stop write —
<code>placePerpStopOrder</code>, <code>cancelPerpStopOrder</code>, <code>cancelPerpStopOrders</code> — takes the
per-pool <code>PerpStopOrderRegistry</code> as a required <code>registry</code> argument. Read it off the
market row: <code>stopRegistry</code> on <code>PerpMarket</code>, and on the market context attached to
perp portfolio orders and fills. <code>null</code> there means the pool has no registry
deployed, so TP/SL is unavailable on it — not that the address is elsewhere.</p>
</li>
<li><p><strong>One call for a market header.</strong> <code>exchange.fetchTicker(symbol)</code> on a perp now
returns <code>markPrice</code>, <code>indexPrice</code>, <code>fundingRate</code>, <code>fundingTimestamp</code> and
<code>openInterest</code> alongside the 24h high/low/volume it already computed from candles.
Those five are chain state, not candle-derived, so before this a header needed a
second read the consumer had to know to make. They are <code>undefined</code> on spot and
binary, and no chain round-trip is spent on a non-perp.</p>
<p>They also survive an indexer outage. The candle read is contained, so a failed
one gives <code>statsState: &quot;unavailable&quot;</code> with the 24h fields absent while these
five still answer. Read <code>statsState</code> before reading a 24h field.</p>
<p><code>fundingRate</code> is on the <strong>same per-8h axis</strong> as <code>fetchFundingRate</code> and
<code>fetchFundingRateHistory</code> — deliberately, because a header on one basis beside a
chart on another is a wrong number that looks right. It is not the per-settlement
amount. For one settlement&#39;s charge, divide by `n = fundingWindowSec /</p>
</li>
</ul>
<p>fundingIntervalSec<code>(8 on every live pool) — and for a HISTORICAL row that caught up   over several intervals, multiply that by the row&#39;s</code>intervalsAccrued<code>, since one   settlement can charge more than one interval&#39;s worth. Both figures ride on </code>info.perp<code>.   </code>markPrice` is omitted rather than reported as 0 when the feed is stale.</p>
<ul>
<li><p><strong>Previewing an order before you send it.</strong>
<code>client.previewPerpOrderMargin({ pool, marginBank, account, isBid, quantity, price })</code>
returns what the pool will actually lock and whether it will accept the order — the
read behind an order form&#39;s &quot;margin required&quot; row and its submit gate.</p>
<p>Do <strong>not</strong> reach for <code>quoteMeetsIMForOrder</code>: it runs with the order&#39;s base margin
treated as already reserved (true on the real path, where <code>lockCollateral</code> runs
first), so called cold it counts the order&#39;s margin nowhere and returns <code>true</code> for
almost any size. <code>meetsPerpImForFill</code> does charge base margin, but neither models
the lock&#39;s <strong>adverse mark-to-entry reserve</strong> — a buy above mark (or sell below)
opens underwater by that gap and the pool reserves it on top of initial margin.
That term is the usual reason a <code>notional × IMF</code>-sized &quot;max&quot; order gets rejected.</p>
<p>Two gates are reported separately because they fail for different reasons and imply
different fixes: <code>hasCollateralForLock</code> (can the lock be taken at all) versus
<code>meetsInitialMargin</code> (does what remains still cover the requirement) — &quot;deposit
more&quot; versus &quot;close something&quot;.</p>
<p>Only the <em>increasing</em> leg locks. An order that nets against an existing position
locks nothing up to <code>effectiveReducingCapacity</code>, which resting opposite-side orders
have already partly spoken for.</p>
<p><strong>Pass <code>autoPull: true</code> when the sender will be the order owner</strong>, and both gates
describe the balance the pool will have topped up to from the wallet rather than the
one already in the bank. <code>topUpRequired</code> is then the wallet spend to show beside the
margin figure, and <code>wallet</code> carries the balance and MarginBank allowance it was
measured against. <code>feeHeadroom</code> appears either way: it is not charged and not part of
<code>lockAmount</code>, only an auto-pull addend that keeps a fresh max-leverage position out of
<code>MarginCall</code> at birth.</p>
<p>A <code>topUpRequired</code> of <code>0n</code> means three different things — no pull needed, or one of the
pool&#39;s declines: a purely reducing order, an account already in debt, or a
voucher-blocked increase. Read it beside <code>unlockedCollateral</code>, not on its own.</p>
<p>Every read is pinned to one block, so the result is a statement about that block;
the adverse-gap term moves with the mark, so re-quote near send time for anything
close to the edge.</p>
</li>
<li><p><strong>Market discovery is a chain read, and &quot;deployed&quot; is not &quot;tradeable.&quot;</strong>
<code>client.listPerpPoolStatuses({ factory })</code> enumerates the
<code>PerpPoolFactory</code> and returns every market with the <strong>two independent gates</strong> that
decide tradeability — they fail for unrelated reasons and both must pass:</p>
<ol>
<li><code>restricted</code> — the market is close-only. Position-increasing orders revert
<code>MarketRestricted</code>; closes, reduces and cancels still work. These stay listed on
purpose (holders must still exit), and it is reversible.</li>
<li><code>registered</code> — the MarginBank has activated the pool. Coming from the factory
only proves a pool is <em>authentic</em>; <code>addPerpPool</code> is what makes it usable. An
unregistered pool rejects every quote view and settlement callback while reading
as an ordinary market from the factory. (<code>getPoolTier</code> is <strong>not</strong> a substitute —
it is itself gated on registration.)</li>
</ol>
<p><code>tradeable</code> folds both together, and <code>listTradeablePerpPools</code> filters to it. You
do not pass a MarginBank: it is a per-network singleton in practice, but each pool
names its own and that is the bank its settlement path uses, so it is read per pool
and returned on every row — ready for the <code>getMarginAccount</code> / <code>getPerpPosition</code>
reads that follow.</p>
<p>Do <strong>not</strong> build a market list from the factory&#39;s raw pool list: it is the
deployment history, so listing it unfiltered presents wound-down markets as
tradeable. Testnet today has 10 pools, four of them restricted <code>SBTC*</code> arena
markets.</p>
<p>This is also more complete than the indexer, whose perp set comes from a curated
manifest — a market deployed after that manifest was written is invisible there and
present here.</p>
</li>
<li><p><strong>Per-market risk parameters are a chain read.</strong> <code>client.getPerpRiskParams(pool)</code>
returns the pool&#39;s frozen config — initial / <strong>maintenance</strong> / close-out margin in
bps, the OI and position caps, and the maker/taker rates. <code>maintenanceMarginBps</code>
is exposed nowhere else (the indexed market row carries only <code>initialMarginBps</code>),
and without it a client can show the real liquidation price of an open position
but not the <strong>projected</strong> one for an order it hasn&#39;t placed. This read never
reverts, so maintenance margin stays available when the mark feed is down —
exactly when you most want to explain a liquidation.</p>
</li>
<li><p><strong>Initial margin is not a constant.</strong> <code>initialMarginBps</code> is only the FLOOR of the
curve: with dynamic IMF enabled the pool scales it with open interest, and
<code>client.getEffectiveImfBps(pool)</code> is the rate actually charged. Sizing an order
off the static base under-margins it whenever OI has pushed the curve up, and the
pool rejects an order the client believed fit. Maintenance margin deliberately
does <strong>not</strong> scale — a liquidation threshold must not move under a position
because the market&#39;s OI grew.</p>
</li>
<li><p><strong>One call for a health walk.</strong> <code>client.getPerpHealthSnapshot(pool)</code> returns
<code>oneBase</code>, mark, projected cumulative funding, the effective IMF and both
thresholds together; the contract added it so a cross-margin walk reads a market
once instead of five times. It returns a discriminated union — an unpriceable
market arrives as <code>{ priceable: false }</code> rather than an all-zero struct, because a
<code>maintenanceMarginBps</code> of <code>0</code> reads as &quot;can never be liquidated&quot;. Narrow on
<code>priceable</code> before touching a field.</p>
</li>
</ul>
<h2 id="linked-wallets-isolated-margin">Linked wallets (isolated margin)</h2>
<p>Isolated margin on perps is one wallet per position. The MarginBank is cross-margin,
so a single account&#39;s collateral backs every position it holds; the only way to give
one position its own collateral bucket is to open it from its own wallet. <code>setIsolated</code>
does not do this — it is single-market confinement, which caps how many markets one
account may touch and changes no margin math.</p>
<p>One wallet per position used to mean one treasury operation per position. The
linked-wallet rail removes that. A wallet links to a <strong>main</strong> wallet, and from then on
the child&#39;s position-increasing orders draw their margin shortfall from the main&#39;s
wallet. One funded main serves every child linked to it.</p>
<p>Two layers, and they answer different questions:</p>
<ul>
<li><code>LinkedWalletRegistry</code> is the <strong>consent</strong> graph. It records who is linked to whom and
grants no authority over funds.</li>
<li><code>MarginBank</code> is the <strong>money</strong> layer, and it is armed separately. Until the bank holds
a registry address the rail is dormant and no child can draw on any main, whatever
the registry says.</li>
</ul>
<h3 id="linking-a-sub-account">Linking a sub-account</h3>
<p>A link takes a two-sided handshake and <strong>both wallets sign</strong>. The main offers, the child
accepts, and no wallet can act for the other:</p>
<div class="code-copy"><pre><code class="language-ts">// The MAIN offers. Its own deposit leaves the standing allowance the rail spends.
await main.trader.depositMargin({ marginBank, amount: fromHuman(1000, 18) });
await main.trader.proposePerpWalletLink({ marginBank, child: childAddress });

// The CHILD accepts, with its own signer and its own transaction.
await child.trader.acceptPerpWalletLink({ marginBank, main: mainAddress });

// The child&#39;s own placement now pulls the shortfall from the main&#39;s wallet.
await child.trader.placePerpOrder({ pool, isBid: true, price, quantity });
</code></pre><button type="button" class="code-copy-btn" data-copy-code aria-live="polite">Copy</button></div>
<p>Pass <code>registry</code>, <code>marginBank</code> or <code>pool</code> to any of the four consent writes. Only
<code>registry</code> skips a chain read: the bank names the registry it treats as authoritative,
so the SDK reads it from the bank rather than a manifest, and a bank holding none throws
<code>NotConfiguredError</code> rather than sending consent nowhere. That read is never cached,
because the bank&#39;s owner can rotate the registry.</p>
<h3 id="how-the-funding-actually-happens">How the funding actually happens</h3>
<p>There is no funding call, and this is the part that surprises people. The main supplies
USDso and an ERC-20 allowance to the <strong>MarginBank</strong>, and then sends nothing. <code>PerpPool</code>
performs the pull inside the child&#39;s own order placement:</p>
<ul>
<li>The child&#39;s own wallet pays first, sized by <code>min(balance, allowance)</code>.</li>
<li>The main&#39;s wallet pays the residual, sized the same way.</li>
<li>The pool&#39;s entire gate is <code>msg.sender == order.owner</code>. An order routed through
<code>placeOrderFor</code>, an operator grant, a router or the stop registry pulls <strong>nothing</strong>,
from either wallet.</li>
</ul>
<p>So the child must send its own orders, and it needs native SOMI for gas. Revoking the
main&#39;s allowance stops the funding without touching the link. Funding does not wait for
link maturity: the rail reads the raw graph, and <code>maturesAt</code> gates ADL netting only.</p>
<p>What a main funds is a <strong>claim, not a gift</strong>. The bank records the funded principal
against the child, and the child&#39;s <code>withdraw</code> frees at most <code>balance - principal</code> — so a
compromised child key can trade the money and lose it, but cannot take it out. The
child&#39;s own money is the junior tranche: a loss eats it first, and a child that
genuinely lost the principal owes nothing.</p>
<h3 id="getting-the-money-back">Getting the money back</h3>
<p>Read the outstanding claim with <code>client.getPerpMainFunding(marginBank, account)</code>. Two
routes send it home, and they differ only in who signs:</p>
<table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Write</th>
<th>Signer</th>
<th>Use it when</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody><tr>
<td><code>trader.repayPerpMainFunding({ amount })</code></td>
<td>the child</td>
<td>the child is finished and returns the money</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><code>trader.recallPerpMainFunding({ child, amount })</code></td>
<td>the payer</td>
<td>the main pulls its capital back</td>
</tr>
</tbody></table>
<p>Both pay the payer the bank snapshotted at funding time, not whoever is linked now, so
an unlink or a re-link in between cannot misroute the money. Both <strong>clamp</strong> the amount
to <code>min(amount, outstanding, present balance)</code>, so over-asking is safe and only a clamp
that reaches zero reverts. Both recover principal and never winnings.</p>
<p><code>trader.unlinkPerpWallet({ counterparty })</code> dissolves the link from either end. It
settles no money, so clear the claim first if the intent is to part cleanly.
<code>PerpsUnlinkGuard</code> vetoes it while the leaver holds open positions and is at
<code>PartialLiquidation</code> or worse; a healthy or flat wallet can always leave.</p>
<h3 id="batching-the-two-steps-that-need-it">Batching the two steps that need it</h3>
<p>Two steps have an ordering dependency inside one transaction, and each has a build-only
twin for it:</p>
<ul>
<li><code>trader.buildAcceptPerpWalletLink</code> — accept, then open the isolated position, in one
signature.</li>
<li><code>trader.buildRepayPerpMainFunding</code> — repay, then withdraw the remainder. The
withdrawal&#39;s own gate reads the claim the repayment clears, so it has to follow.</li>
</ul>
<p>The other four writes are standalone administrative calls and have no twin.</p>
<h3 id="which-read-answers-which-question">Which read answers which question</h3>
<p>The six chain reads are live contract STATE — a value as of a block, which no log can
reconstruct. The history is the opposite: the contracts expose no getter for any of it,
so the events in the chain&#39;s logs are the only record, and the indexer is what makes
them queryable.</p>
<table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Question</th>
<th>Read</th>
<th>Tier</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody><tr>
<td>Is the rail on at all on this deployment?</td>
<td><code>client.getPerpLinkedWalletRegistry(marginBank)</code></td>
<td>chain</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Is a main eligible to fund this account, and which?</td>
<td><code>client.quotePerpFundingPayer(marginBank, account)</code></td>
<td>chain</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>What is outstanding, and to whom?</td>
<td><code>client.getPerpMainFunding(marginBank, account)</code></td>
<td>chain</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>What can a wallet actually contribute right now?</td>
<td><code>client.getPerpWalletPullCapacity(marginBank, wallet)</code></td>
<td>chain</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Who is in my group, and when did it mature?</td>
<td><code>client.getPerpWalletLinkage(registry, wallet)</code></td>
<td>chain</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Which children does this main have?</td>
<td><code>client.listPerpLinkedChildren(registry, main)</code></td>
<td>chain</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>How many children may one main hold?</td>
<td><code>client.getPerpMaxLinkedChildren(registry)</code></td>
<td>chain</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>How did it get to this state?</td>
<td>the three <code>list*</code> ledgers below</td>
<td>indexer</td>
</tr>
</tbody></table>
<p><strong>Eligibility is not a prediction.</strong> <code>quotePerpFundingPayer</code> takes no order and no
sender, so it cannot say whether a given order will debit the main. Four things stop an
eligible main from paying anything: the order needs no top-up, the child&#39;s own wallet
covers the whole pull, the order is routed by someone other than its owner, or the main
has no spendable capacity. Size the order with <code>client.previewPerpOrderMargin({ autoPull: true })</code> and read its <code>mainWalletPull</code> for the amount that would actually
move.</p>
<p><code>quotePerpFundingPayer</code> returns a discriminated union rather than an address, because
the contract&#39;s single zero collapses three situations a UI must not render alike.
Narrow on <code>funded</code> first:</p>
<div class="code-copy"><pre><code class="language-ts">const payer = await client.quotePerpFundingPayer(marginBank, account);
if (payer.funded) {
  // A main is ELIGIBLE to cover what this account&#39;s own wallet cannot. Whether any
  // given order debits it is `previewPerpOrderMargin({ autoPull: true }).mainWalletPull`.
  console.log(&quot;eligible payer&quot;, payer.payer);
} else if (payer.reason === &quot;unlinked&quot;) {
  // The one case the user can fix, by linking.
} else if (payer.reason === &quot;dormant&quot;) {
  // The rail is off for everyone here. Linking would not help.
} else {
  // &quot;isMain&quot; — linked, but funding flows main-&gt;child only.
}
</code></pre><button type="button" class="code-copy-btn" data-copy-code aria-live="polite">Copy</button></div>
<p><code>getPerpMainFunding</code> reports the claim and the payer <strong>snapshotted at funding time</strong>,
which is not necessarily whoever is linked now. When the two disagree, the snapshot is
who gets repaid. It also carries <code>withdrawableFromPrincipal</code>, which is always <code>0n</code> — a
field rather than prose because &quot;why can I not withdraw my balance&quot; is the commonest
question this surface has to answer.</p>
<h3 id="finding-an-incoming-proposal">Finding an incoming proposal</h3>
<p>The registry keeps pending proposals in a hashed map with <strong>no getter</strong>. So a child
cannot discover from any chain read that a main has offered it a link, and
<code>client.listPerpWalletLinkEvents</code> is the only evidence such an offer exists.</p>
<p>That list yields <strong>candidates, not accepts that will succeed</strong>. <code>acceptLink</code> applies
five live-state guards, and each is a current-state rather than an ever-happened
question:</p>
<ol>
<li>An offer stands only while the pair&#39;s newest row is the <code>Proposed</code> itself. Mind the
direction: an unlink clears the pending proposal in <strong>both</strong> directions, so an
<code>Unlinked</code> naming this wallet as the main can retire an offer it holds as the child.</li>
<li>The child must be free right now (<code>AlreadyLinked</code>). Accepting one main leaves the
losing mains&#39; proposals in storage, dead only while that link stands.</li>
<li>The main must not itself be a child right now (<code>CallerIsChild</code>).</li>
<li>The accepting wallet must not itself be a main (<code>CallerIsMain</code>).</li>
<li>The main must be under its child cap (<code>MaxChildrenReached</code>).</li>
</ol>
<p>All five are <strong>derivable in principle</strong> from a complete <code>Linked</code> / <code>Unlinked</code> replay —
except the cap, whose value is owner-tunable and whose <code>MaxChildrenUpdated</code> event is
deliberately not subscribed. In practice a paginated page of rows is not a complete
replay, which is the real reason not to decide an accept off this list.</p>
<p>So confirm on chain. <code>client.getPerpWalletLinkage(registry, wallet)</code> settles guards 2,
3 and 4 — read it on both parties, since <code>main</code> being non-zero means the wallet is
already in a group. It does <strong>not</strong> carry the cap: that is
<code>client.getPerpMaxLinkedChildren(registry)</code> against
<code>client.listPerpLinkedChildren(registry, main)</code>.</p>
<h3 id="the-two-funding-ledgers-which-must-not-be-summed">The two funding ledgers, which must not be summed</h3>
<p>Every proposal, link, pull, settle and return is indexed, because the chain keeps no
history of any of it:</p>
<div class="code-copy"><pre><code class="language-ts">const links = await client.listPerpWalletLinkEvents({ child: account }); // Proposed | ProposalCancelled | Linked | Unlinked
const pulls = await client.listPerpMarginPulls({ account, source: &quot;Main&quot; }); // the POOL side — names the order
const claims = await client.listPerpMainFundingEvents({ account }); // the BANK side — carries the running claim
</code></pre><button type="button" class="code-copy-btn" data-copy-code aria-live="polite">Copy</button></div>
<p><strong>A MAIN-funded leg emits a row on each side for the same wei.</strong> Adding
<code>listPerpMarginPulls</code> to <code>listPerpMainFundingEvents</code> therefore double-counts every
main-funded transfer. Read one or the other, never both as one total, and pick by the
question: the pool side names the <strong>order</strong>, the bank side carries the <strong>claim</strong>.</p>
<p>The overlap is exactly the main legs, and no more. So <code>PerpMainFundingEvent</code> is the
main legs only, while <code>PerpMarginPull</code> is both.</p>
<p>An own-wallet pull also has a bank-side twin. The bank&#39;s <code>DepositedFor</code> is a margin
event with <code>kind: &quot;DepositFor&quot;</code>, and it carries the same amount as
<code>PerpMarginPull(source: &quot;OwnWallet&quot;)</code>. Do not add the two together. The pull names the
order; the margin event does not.</p>
<p>One placement can produce two <code>PerpMarginPull</code> rows — an <code>OwnWallet</code> leg and a <code>Main</code>
leg, in that order — because the child&#39;s own wallet contributes what it can before the
main is touched. Filter on <code>source: &quot;Main&quot;</code> to see only what a main actually paid for.</p>
<p>On <code>PerpMainFundingEvent</code>, a <code>Settled</code> row moved no cash: it records the child&#39;s losses
discharging part of the claim, so its <code>amount</code> is <code>null</code> while
<code>outstandingPrincipal</code> still drops. Fold by <code>kind</code> rather than summing <code>amount</code>.</p>
<h2 id="credit-vouchers-house-money">Credit vouchers (house money)</h2>
<p>A credit voucher is a one-time grant of collateral into a new account&#39;s MarginBank
balance. The grant is non-withdrawable. The account can trade with it and can withdraw
profit above it. A <code>VoucherDistributor</code> holds the budget and the campaign rules, and the
bank names the live distributor, so no deployment address is needed.</p>
<table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Question</th>
<th>Read</th>
<th>Tier</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody><tr>
<td>Is a campaign live, and what does it pay?</td>
<td><code>client.getPerpVoucherCampaign(marginBank)</code></td>
<td>chain</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Can this account claim now? If not, why?</td>
<td><code>client.getPerpVoucherEligibility(marginBank, account)</code></td>
<td>chain</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>How much house money does it hold?</td>
<td><code>client.getPerpVoucher(marginBank, account)</code></td>
<td>chain</td>
</tr>
</tbody></table>
<p>Check eligibility before you claim. A failed claim still mines and costs gas:</p>
<div class="code-copy"><pre><code class="language-ts">const eligibility = await client.getPerpVoucherEligibility(marginBank, account);
if (eligibility.eligible) {
  await trader.claimPerpVoucher({ marginBank });
} else {
  // &quot;dormant&quot; | &quot;notStarted&quot; | &quot;ended&quot; | &quot;notFlat&quot; | &quot;mainFunded&quot; | &quot;alreadyClaimed&quot; | &quot;budgetExhausted&quot;
  console.log(&quot;cannot claim:&quot;, eligibility.reason);
}
</code></pre><button type="button" class="code-copy-btn" data-copy-code aria-live="polite">Copy</button></div>
<p>Rules to know before you build on it:</p>
<ul>
<li><strong>One claim per address, for the distributor&#39;s life.</strong> A new campaign window does not
reset it.</li>
<li><strong>The account must be flat to claim.</strong> No positions and no resting perp orders.</li>
<li><strong>The grant confines position increases.</strong> Only allowlisted markets, at no more than
the voucher leverage cap, and the account cannot change its own leverage setting.
<code>getPerpLeverage</code> and <code>previewPerpOrderMargin</code> already apply these rules.</li>
<li><strong>Own funds can mix in.</strong> A voucher account can still deposit, and an order placed
with an ERC-20 allowance to the bank can pull wallet collateral in. To keep an account
on house money only, do not ask it for that allowance.</li>
<li><strong>The grant expires.</strong> After <code>expiresAt</code>, anyone can sweep the remaining floor back to
the distributor, but only while the account is flat. A sweep never forces a close.</li>
<li><strong>Each grant and each sweep is indexed.</strong> A grant appears in <code>client.getMarginEvents</code> as
<code>kind: &quot;Credited&quot;</code>, and a sweep as <code>kind: &quot;CreditReclaimed&quot;</code>, an outflow whose
<code>granter</code> is the address that swept it. A sweep of an account with no positive
balance left has <code>amount</code> 0.</li>
</ul>
<p><code>trader.buildClaimPerpVoucher</code> returns the unsigned claim, so a claim and the first order
can go out as one transaction. The claim must execute first.</p>
<h2 id="history-indexer">History (indexer)</h2>
<p>The CURRENT position/margin is the chain read above; the <strong>append-only history</strong>
the chain doesn&#39;t expose is indexed (perp account plane), all one-shot indexer
reads:</p>
<div class="code-copy"><pre><code class="language-ts">const funding = await client.getFundingPayments(account, { pool, limit: 50 }); // signed funding paid/received
const margin = await client.getMarginEvents(account, { limit: 50 }); // deposit/withdraw/lock/unlock/credit/reclaim
const liqs = await client.listLiquidations({ party: account, pool }); // liquidation events involving this wallet
const changes = await client.listPerpPositionChanges({ account, pool }); // every position update, with its realized PnL
const fillChanges = await client.listPerpPositionChangesForFills(account, fills); // the update each of the account&#39;s fills made
const rates = await client.listFundingRateHistory(pool, { from, to }); // per-pool funding-rate series
const candles = await client.listFundingRateCandles(pool, 3600, { from, to }); // 1h/4h/1d rollups for charting
const oi = await client.getOpenInterestHistory(pool); // per-pool open-interest series
</code></pre><button type="button" class="code-copy-btn" data-copy-code aria-live="polite">Copy</button></div>
<h3 id="realized-pnl-per-close">Realized PnL per close</h3>
<p>This section explains how to read the result of one closed trade. The position row from
<code>listPerpPositions</code> keeps only the latest state, so it cannot show one trade.</p>
<p><code>client.listPerpPositionChanges</code> returns one row for each <code>MarginBank.PositionUpdated</code>.
MarginBank emits one for each order-book fill, for each auto-deleveraging or market wind-down leg, and for
both sides of a liquidation takeover. So the history covers every way a position closes,
also liquidations that leave no fill.</p>
<ul>
<li>Each row has the signed size before and after, the size it closed, the entry price
before and after, and the PnL of that update alone. All values are exact <code>bigint</code> raw
units.</li>
<li><code>kind</code> says what the update did: <code>OPEN</code>, <code>INCREASE</code>, <code>REDUCE</code>, <code>CLOSE</code> or <code>FLIP</code>. Pass
<code>kinds: [&quot;REDUCE&quot;, &quot;CLOSE&quot;, &quot;FLIP&quot;]</code> to get only the updates that realized PnL.</li>
<li>One order that fills across several book levels makes several rows. Group the rows by
<code>account</code>, <code>pool</code> and <code>orderId</code>, and sum their <code>realizedPnl</code> and <code>closedSize</code>. Do not
group by <code>txHash</code> alone: one transaction can hold both parties&#39; rows and several
orders. Rows with no <code>orderId</code> (liquidation takeover, ADL, wind-down) are one close each.</li>
<li>A full close reports a zero entry price. <code>entryPriceBefore</code> keeps the entry that the
close realized against, and <code>exitPrice</code> the price it closed at, also for a liquidation,
ADL or wind-down close with no fill.</li>
<li>The PnL is price PnL only. Fees (<code>listPerpFees</code>) and funding (<code>getFundingPayments</code>) are
separate records.</li>
</ul>
<p>To label a trade history, <code>client.listPerpPositionChangesForFills(account, fills)</code> pairs
each of the account&#39;s fills with the update that fill made, so a row can say Open Long,
Close Short or show a flip. Pass one page of the account&#39;s fills, at most 1,000. The result
has one <code>PerpPositionChange</code> or <code>null</code> for each fill, in the same order. <code>null</code> means the
fill is not the account&#39;s, is not a perp fill, or its update is not indexed yet. An indexer
that predates position changes throws <code>PerpPositionChangesUnavailableError</code> instead: show
each fill&#39;s own side until it is reindexed.</p>
<p><code>IndexedPerpPosition.lastUpdateRealizedPnl</code> is the running total of the same deltas for the
current position. It restarts from zero when a flat position reopens or the position flips side.</p>
<p><code>listFundingRateHistory</code> / <code>getOpenInterestHistory</code> are the append-only
counterparts to the overwrite-only <code>fundingRate</code> / <code>openInterest</code> fields on the
perp <code>Market</code> row (which only carry the latest value). <code>getFundingRateHistory</code> is
the deprecated alias of the first; it forwards verbatim. <code>listFundingRateHistory</code>
sorts on <code>timestamp</code>, then <code>blockNumber</code>, then <code>id</code>, all in the direction of
<code>order</code>. Rows that share a timestamp therefore come back in the same order on
every read.</p>
<p>Liquidation history is served by the <code>LiquidationEngine</code> subscription, which is
live — the contract is deployed and indexed from block 436,735,800. There is no
<code>MarginBank</code>-sourced fallback: <code>MarginBank.Liquidated</code> was deleted from the
protocol, so the rows that claim used to describe cannot exist. Per-position
detail comes from <code>LiquidationEngine.PositionLiquidated</code>, and the waterfall&#39;s
other outcomes (ADL, takeover, close-out, the residual and declined-coverage
markers) arrive as sibling rows sharing a <code>txHash</code> — read <code>kind</code> to tell them
apart, and read the TSDoc on <code>badDebt</code> / <code>insuranceCovered</code> / <code>deficit</code> /
<code>coverageDeclined</code> before aggregating any of them, because only the flows are
summable.</p>
<h3 id="reading-a-liquidation39s-stage">Reading a liquidation&#39;s stage</h3>
<p><code>AccountLiquidated</code> rows carry <code>stageReached</code>. It tells you how far down the
waterfall a liquidation went. The number needs a mapping, because the protocol
enum is 0-indexed with six members while the published waterfall numbers its
stages 1 to 6:</p>
<table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th><code>stageReached</code></th>
<th>Enum member</th>
<th>Published stage</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody><tr>
<td>0</td>
<td><code>OrderCancellation</code></td>
<td>before stage 3 — cancelling the account&#39;s resting orders alone restored health</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>1</td>
<td><code>CLOBPartial</code></td>
<td>stage 3</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>2</td>
<td><code>BidderTakeover</code></td>
<td>stage 4</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>3</td>
<td><code>InsuranceFund</code></td>
<td>stage 5</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>4</td>
<td><code>ADL</code></td>
<td>stage 6</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>5</td>
<td><code>Deferred</code></td>
<td>not a stage — stage 3 hit its per-block rate limit, and stages 4 to 6 were not consulted. Ships dormant, so it does not appear yet</td>
</tr>
</tbody></table>
<p>Published stages 1 (Healthy) and 2 (Margin Call) are account states, not actions.
The engine cannot reach them, so no value maps to them.</p>
<p>Two rules govern the number, and neither is visible in the value itself.</p>
<p><strong>It names the deepest stage that acted, not the deepest stage consulted.</strong> A
stage that ran and moved nothing does not promote it. So a <code>BidderTakeover</code> (2)
row sitting beside a <code>BadDebtAbsorbed</code> row is consistent. It means the insurance
fund was asked and paid nothing. <code>BadDebtAbsorbed</code> fires on the request, not on
the payment, so read <code>insuranceCovered</code> on that row to see whether the fund
actually paid. A market the fund does not cover produces exactly this shape: the
loss falls through as <code>ResidualBadDebt</code>.</p>
<p><strong><code>CLOBPartial</code> (1) is a fall-through marker.</strong> It means stages 4 to 6 were
consulted and none of them acted. It does not prove stage 3 filled anything.
Against an empty book you get <code>CLOBPartial</code> with a <code>positionsProcessed</code> of 0,
which means the account&#39;s orders were cancelled and no position was closed. Read
<code>positionsProcessed</code> with it:</p>
<div class="code-copy"><pre><code class="language-ts">const rows = await client.getLiquidations({ account });
const summary = rows.find((r) =&gt; r.kind === &quot;AccountLiquidated&quot;);
// A real CLOB partial fill, not an empty-book no-op.
const clobFilled = summary?.stageReached === 1 &amp;&amp; Number(summary.positionsProcessed) &gt; 0;
</code></pre><button type="button" class="code-copy-btn" data-copy-code aria-live="polite">Copy</button></div>
<p><code>counterparty</code> on a <code>BadDebtAbsorbed</code> row names the fund the engine asked. The
address is recorded even when <code>insuranceCovered</code> is 0, so it does not mean the
fund paid either.</p>
<p><code>marginStatusAfter</code> needs the same care. A flat account that still owes bad debt
reports <code>CloseOut</code> (3) so the debt stays visible to monitoring. That is a
terminal state, not an account that is still liquidatable. A <code>ResidualBadDebt</code>
row in the same transaction identifies it.</p>
<p>If you query the indexer&#39;s GraphQL directly rather than through the SDK, note
that its own field descriptions still carry the older wording for <code>kind</code>,
<code>counterparty</code> and <code>stageReached</code>. Correcting them changes the served schema and
so needs a reindex; it is tracked separately. This section is the current
reference.</p>
<h2 id="watch-the-perp-plane-liquidations-funding-settlement">Watch the perp plane (liquidations + funding settlement)</h2>
<p><code>watchMarket(pool)</code> gives a perp pool&#39;s book, funding RATE and open interest.
<code>watchPerpMarkets()</code> gives the other half: the liquidation waterfall and
per-account funding settlement, with the same snapshot-then-live guarantee.
Neither implies the other, so a UI showing both opens both.</p>
<div class="code-copy"><pre><code class="language-ts">const plane = await client.watchPerpMarkets();

const liqs = client.listLiveLiquidations({ party: account, limit: 50 });
const paid = client.listLiveFundingPayments({ account, limit: 50 });

const unsubscribe = client.subscribeLive(() =&gt; {
  // re-read; results are memoized per store version
});

// Teardown: the listener and the watch are released separately.
unsubscribe();
plane.stop();
</code></pre><button type="button" class="code-copy-btn" data-copy-code aria-live="polite">Copy</button></div>
<p>The rows are the SAME <code>LiquidationEvent</code> and <code>FundingPayment</code> types
<code>listLiquidations</code> and <code>getFundingPayments</code> serve, in the same newest-first
order, so the two merge without translation. The live window is hydrated from
the newest indexed rows, so it OVERLAPS the newest indexed page: merge by <code>id</code>,
